# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q001

## title
Which of the following is NOT a content of the deep perineal pouch in the male?

## question
Which of the following is NOT a content of the deep perineal pouch in the male?

## subject
androl

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Bulb of the penis

## explanation_a
Correct. The bulb of the penis lies in the superficial perineal pouch, attached to the inferior surface of the perineal membrane and covered by bulbospongiosus; it is not a content of the deep perineal pouch, which lies between the two fascial layers of the urogenital diaphragm above the perineal membrane. This is a common source of confusion because the bulb sits immediately below the deep pouch, on the other side of the perineal membrane.

## answer_b
Membranous urethra

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The membranous urethra pierces the deep perineal pouch as it passes from the prostate to the bulb of the penis, surrounded by the external urethral sphincter.

## answer_c
Bulbourethral (Cowper) glands

## explanation_c
Incorrect. The paired bulbourethral (Cowper) glands lie within the substance of the deep perineal pouch, embedded in the external urethral sphincter, with ducts that cross the perineal membrane to open into the bulbous urethra.

## answer_d
External urethral sphincter (sphincter urethrae)

## explanation_d
Incorrect. The external urethral sphincter (sphincter urethrae) is a genuine skeletal-muscle content of the deep perineal pouch, encircling the membranous urethra.

## topic
Male reproductive anatomy

## subtopic

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q002

## title
The prostatic urethra emerges from the prostate at its apex through which surface?

## question
The prostatic urethra emerges from the prostate at its apex through which surface?

## subject
androl

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Anterior surface

## explanation_a
Correct. The urethra enters the prostate at the middle of its base (posteriorly placed) and runs through the gland to leave at the apex through its anteroinferior part, so the urethra emerges anteriorly relative to the apex. This anterior-apex exit point is a useful landmark during radical prostatectomy, where the urethra must be divided cleanly at the apex.

## answer_b
Posterior surface

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The posterior surface of the prostate is related to the rectum and is where the ejaculatory ducts enter, not where the urethra exits.

## answer_c
Base (superior surface)

## explanation_c
Incorrect. The base of the prostate is where the urethra enters (from the bladder neck), not where it emerges.

## answer_d
Lateral surface

## explanation_d
Incorrect. The lateral surfaces of the prostate are related to levator ani, not to the urethra course.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q003

## title
Which lobe of the prostate lies posterior to the upper (proximal) part of the prostatic urethra, between the urethra and the ejaculatory ducts?

## question
Which lobe of the prostate lies posterior to the upper (proximal) part of the prostatic urethra, between the urethra and the ejaculatory ducts?

## subject
androl

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Median (middle) lobe

## explanation_a
Correct. The median (middle) lobe is a wedge of glandular tissue that lies between the urethra anteriorly and the two ejaculatory ducts posteriorly, immediately below the bladder neck; its enlargement in benign prostatic hyperplasia can project into the bladder as a "ball valve" and obstruct the internal urethral orifice. This wedge is the site of the classic bladder-neck "ball-valve" obstruction seen when the median lobe enlarges in benign prostatic hyperplasia.

## answer_b
Posterior lobe

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The posterior lobe lies posterior to the urethra as a whole and below the ejaculatory ducts, not sandwiched between the upper urethra and the ducts.

## answer_c
Lateral lobes

## explanation_c
Incorrect. The two lateral lobes flank the urethra on either side and form the bulk of the gland; they are not the wedge specifically between urethra and ejaculatory ducts.

## answer_d
Anterior lobe (isthmus)

## explanation_d
Incorrect. The anterior lobe (isthmus) is a small fibromuscular band anterior to the urethra with little or no glandular tissue.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q004

## title
The lumbosacral trunk, which descends into the pelvis to join the sacral plexus, is formed by the ventral rami of which spinal nerves?

## question
The lumbosacral trunk, which descends into the pelvis to join the sacral plexus, is formed by the ventral rami of which spinal nerves?

## subject
msk

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
L4 and L5

## explanation_a
Correct. The lumbosacral trunk is formed by the smaller, descending part of the L4 ventral ramus joining the whole L5 ventral ramus; it crosses the pelvic brim anterior to the sacroiliac joint to join S1 and contribute to the sacral plexus, linking the lumbar and sacral plexuses functionally. This partial contribution explains why the lumbosacral trunk is often drawn as the structural bridge linking the two plexuses on diagrams.

## answer_b
L2 and L3

## explanation_b
Incorrect. L2 and L3 ventral rami contribute to the lumbar plexus proper (e.g., obturator and femoral nerves), not the lumbosacral trunk.

## answer_c
L3 and L4

## explanation_c
Incorrect. L3 contributes to the lumbar plexus, not the lumbosacral trunk; only part of L4 descends to join L5.

## answer_d
S1 and S2

## explanation_d
Incorrect. S1 and S2 are sacral plexus roots that the lumbosacral trunk joins, not the rami that form the trunk itself.

## topic
Pelvic wall anatomy

## subtopic

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q005

## title
Which of the following arteries arises from the anterior division of the internal iliac artery?

## question
Which of the following arteries arises from the anterior division of the internal iliac artery?

## subject
msk

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Obturator artery

## explanation_a
Correct. The obturator artery is a visceral/parietal branch of the anterior division of the internal iliac artery; it runs forward on the lateral pelvic wall to leave the pelvis through the obturator canal. Remembering "anterior division = mostly visceral pelvic branches, posterior division = mostly parietal wall branches" helps sort obturator from its posterior-division counterparts quickly.

## answer_b
Iliolumbar artery

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The iliolumbar artery arises from the posterior division and ascends to supply the iliacus and psoas region.

## answer_c
Lateral sacral artery

## explanation_c
Incorrect. The lateral sacral arteries arise from the posterior division and supply the sacral canal contents and overlying skin.

## answer_d
Superior gluteal artery

## explanation_d
Incorrect. The superior gluteal artery is the largest branch of the posterior division, leaving the pelvis above piriformis.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q006

## title
Which artery anastomoses with the uterine artery to help supply the vagina?

## question
Which artery anastomoses with the uterine artery to help supply the vagina?

## subject
gyn

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Vaginal artery

## explanation_a
Correct. The vaginal artery (usually a branch of the anterior division of the internal iliac, or of the uterine artery itself) descends on the vagina and anastomoses with the descending cervical branch of the uterine artery, forming a longitudinal anastomotic channel ("azygos artery of the vagina") that supplies the vaginal walls. This anastomotic chain running down the lateral vaginal wall is sometimes called the azygos artery of the vagina.

## answer_b
Ovarian artery

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The ovarian artery anastomoses with the ascending (tubal/ovarian) branch of the uterine artery near the ovary and tube, not specifically to supply the vagina.

## answer_c
Obturator artery

## explanation_c
Incorrect. The obturator artery supplies the obturator region and hip muscles, not the vagina.

## answer_d
Inferior epigastric artery

## explanation_d
Incorrect. The inferior epigastric artery supplies the anterior abdominal wall, unrelated to vaginal supply.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q007

## title
Which structure is directly related to the posterior surface of the prostate?

## question
Which structure is directly related to the posterior surface of the prostate?

## subject
androl

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Rectum

## explanation_a
Correct. The posterior surface of the prostate is separated from the rectal ampulla only by the rectovesical (Denonvilliers) fascia, which is why the prostate is palpable and biopsied through the anterior rectal wall on digital rectal examination. This single-fascial-layer separation is also why locally advanced prostate cancer can directly invade the rectal wall.

## answer_b
Urinary bladder

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The urinary bladder is related to the base (superior surface) of the prostate, not its posterior surface.

## answer_c
Pubic symphysis

## explanation_c
Incorrect. The pubic symphysis lies anterior to the prostate, connected via the puboprostatic ligaments, not posteriorly.

## answer_d
Levator ani muscle

## explanation_d
Incorrect. Levator ani is related to the inferolateral surfaces of the prostate, not specifically its posterior surface.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q008

## title
The space bounded above by the superior fascial layer of the urogenital diaphragm and below by the perineal membrane (inferior fascia) is called the?

## question
The space bounded above by the superior fascial layer of the urogenital diaphragm and below by the perineal membrane (inferior fascia) is called the?

## subject
androl

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Deep perineal pouch

## explanation_a
Correct. The deep perineal pouch is the closed compartment sandwiched between the superior and inferior fascial layers of the urogenital diaphragm; it houses the membranous urethra, external urethral sphincter, deep transverse perineal muscles and the bulbourethral glands in the male. The mnemonic "deep is above the membrane, superficial is below it" keeps the two perineal pouches from being reversed on exam.

## answer_b
Superficial perineal pouch

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The superficial perineal pouch lies below the perineal membrane, between it and Colles' fascia, and contains the root structures of the external genitalia (bulb and crura), not the space above the membrane.

## answer_c
Ischiorectal (ischioanal) fossa

## explanation_c
Incorrect. The ischiorectal fossa is a fat-filled space lateral to the anal canal, unrelated to the urogenital diaphragm's two fascial layers.

## answer_d
Retropubic (Retzius) space

## explanation_d
Incorrect. The retropubic space lies between the pubic bones and the bladder, well above the perineum.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q009

## title
Which of the following is NOT a content of the spermatic cord?

## question
Which of the following is NOT a content of the spermatic cord?

## subject
androl

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Ilioinguinal nerve

## explanation_a
Correct. The ilioinguinal nerve travels through the inguinal canal alongside the spermatic cord, lying on its anterior surface outside its fascial coverings, and emerges through the superficial inguinal ring separately; it is not enclosed within the cord's coverings and so is not classed as one of its contents. Only the genital branch of the genitofemoral nerve pierces the deep inguinal ring to travel inside the cord itself, which is the detail that separates it from the ilioinguinal nerve.

## answer_b
Genital branch of the genitofemoral nerve

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The genital branch of the genitofemoral nerve pierces the deep inguinal ring to run within the cord (supplying cremaster and scrotal skin) and is a true content.

## answer_c
Testicular artery

## explanation_c
Incorrect. The testicular artery, a direct branch of the abdominal aorta, is a core content of the spermatic cord.

## answer_d
Pampiniform venous plexus

## explanation_d
Incorrect. The pampiniform venous plexus, draining the testis, is a major content of the cord.

## topic
Male reproductive anatomy

## subtopic
Spermatic cord

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q010

## title
Which of the following nerves is NOT a branch of the sacral plexus?

## question
Which of the following nerves is NOT a branch of the sacral plexus?

## subject
msk

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Obturator nerve

## explanation_a
Correct. The obturator nerve (L2-L4) is a branch of the lumbar plexus, formed in the substance of psoas major and leaving the pelvis through the obturator canal; it does not arise from the sacral plexus. Remembering the obturator nerve as "L2-L4, lumbar plexus, exits via the obturator canal" separates it cleanly from the sacral plexus branches that exit via the greater sciatic foramen.

## answer_b
Sciatic nerve

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The sciatic nerve (L4-S3) is the largest branch of the sacral plexus, leaving the pelvis below piriformis through the greater sciatic foramen.

## answer_c
Pudendal nerve

## explanation_c
Incorrect. The pudendal nerve (S2-S4) is a sacral plexus branch supplying the perineum.

## answer_d
Superior gluteal nerve

## explanation_d
Incorrect. The superior gluteal nerve (L4-S1) is a sacral plexus branch leaving above piriformis to supply the gluteus medius, minimus and tensor fasciae latae.

Pelvic wall anatomy

## subtopic

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q011

## title
Which artery is a branch of the posterior division of the internal iliac artery?

## question
Which artery is a branch of the posterior division of the internal iliac artery?

## subject
msk

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Superior gluteal artery

## explanation_a
Correct. The superior gluteal artery is the largest and most direct continuation of the posterior division of the internal iliac artery, leaving the pelvis above piriformis through the greater sciatic foramen to supply the gluteal muscles. Because it is the only major artery to leave through the greater sciatic foramen above piriformis, the superior gluteal artery is a useful landmark for that muscle during posterior hip surgery.

## answer_b
Obturator artery

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The obturator artery arises from the anterior division, supplying the medial thigh via the obturator canal.

## answer_c
Inferior vesical artery

## explanation_c
Incorrect. The inferior vesical artery (male) is an anterior-division visceral branch supplying the bladder base, prostate and seminal vesicles.

## answer_d
Uterine artery

## explanation_d
Incorrect. The uterine artery is an anterior-division visceral branch in the female.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q012

## title
The seminal vesicles are located between which two structures?

## question
The seminal vesicles are located between which two structures?

## subject
androl

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
The rectum (posteriorly) and the base of the urinary bladder (anteriorly)

## explanation_a
Correct. Each seminal vesicle is a coiled tubular pouch lying obliquely above the prostate, between the base of the bladder in front and the rectum behind, related medially to the ampulla of the vas deferens; this rectovesical position is why the vesicles can be palpated on digital rectal examination in disease. This position also explains why seminal vesicle pathology (e.g. invasion by prostate cancer) can be assessed by transrectal ultrasound or MRI.

## answer_b
The prostate and the rectum

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The prostate lies inferior to the seminal vesicles, not on the other side of them from the rectum.

## answer_c
The urinary bladder and the pubic symphysis

## explanation_c
Incorrect. The pubic symphysis lies well anterior to the bladder itself, not adjacent to the seminal vesicles.

## answer_d
The ureter and the vas deferens

## explanation_d
Incorrect. The ureter passes near, but is not one of the two structures the seminal vesicle is sandwiched between; the vas deferens lies medial to the vesicle, not on an opposite side.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q013

## title
The penis is embryologically homologous to which female structure?

## question
The penis is embryologically homologous to which female structure?

## subject
gyn

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Clitoris

## explanation_a
Correct. Both the penis and the clitoris develop from the same indifferent primordium, the genital tubercle, which elongates under androgen influence in the male to form the penis and remains relatively small in the female to form the clitoris; both retain corpora cavernosa-like erectile tissue and a glans. Recognising this shared origin also explains why both structures can develop erectile engorgement and share some innervation.

## answer_b
Labia majora

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The labia majora are homologous to the scrotum, both developing from the labioscrotal (genital) swellings.

## answer_c
Labia minora

## explanation_c
Incorrect. The labia minora are homologous to the ventral penile shaft skin around the spongy urethra, both developing from the urogenital (urethral) folds, not to the penis as a whole.

## answer_d
Bartholin's (greater vestibular) glands

## explanation_d
Incorrect. Bartholin's glands are homologous to the bulbourethral (Cowper) glands, not to the penis.

## topic

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q014

## title
The seminal vesicles receive their arterial supply mainly from which artery?

## question
The seminal vesicles receive their arterial supply mainly from which artery?

## subject
androl

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Middle rectal artery

## explanation_a
Correct. The seminal vesicles are supplied mainly by branches of the middle rectal artery (with a variable contribution from the inferior vesical artery), both being visceral branches of the anterior division of the internal iliac artery that reach the vesicles from below and behind. This dual supply is why the middle rectal artery is deliberately preserved or ligated with care during pelvic surgery near the seminal vesicles.

## answer_b
Superior rectal artery

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The superior rectal artery, a continuation of the inferior mesenteric artery, supplies the upper rectum, not the seminal vesicles.

## answer_c
Internal pudendal artery

## explanation_c
Incorrect. The internal pudendal artery supplies the perineum (external genitalia, anal canal), not the seminal vesicles.

## answer_d
Obturator artery

## explanation_d
Incorrect. The obturator artery supplies the medial thigh and hip region via the obturator canal.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q015

## title
Which of the following is NOT considered a content of the spermatic cord as it traverses the inguinal canal?

## question
Which of the following is NOT considered a content of the spermatic cord as it traverses the inguinal canal?

## subject
androl

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
A single testicular vein

## explanation_a
Correct. Within the inguinal canal and scrotum, testicular venous drainage exists as the multiple interconnecting channels of the pampiniform plexus; these veins condense into a single testicular vein only after the cord terminates near the deep inguinal ring, so a "single testicular vein" as such is not a content of the cord itself. This distinction matters clinically because varicocele repair targets the plexus of veins within the cord, not a single named vein.

## answer_b
Pampiniform venous plexus

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The pampiniform venous plexus is the actual venous content of the spermatic cord, providing countercurrent heat exchange that keeps the testis cool.

## answer_c
Testicular artery

## explanation_c
Incorrect. The testicular artery is a core content of the spermatic cord, running within the cremasteric and internal spermatic fascia.

## answer_d
Vas deferens

## explanation_d
Incorrect. The vas deferens is the defining content of the spermatic cord, running along its posterior aspect.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q016

## title
Which lobe of the prostate lies related to the urogenital diaphragm inferiorly and to the ejaculatory ducts superiorly as they enter the gland?

## question
Which lobe of the prostate lies related to the urogenital diaphragm inferiorly and to the ejaculatory ducts superiorly as they enter the gland?

## subject
androl

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Posterior lobe

## explanation_a
Correct. The posterior lobe lies behind the urethra and below the ejaculatory ducts, extending down to rest on the urogenital diaphragm; it is the lobe most accessible to digital rectal examination and the site of origin of most prostate cancers. This posterior, sub-ductal position is also why most prostate adenocarcinomas, which characteristically arise in the posterior (peripheral) zone, are detected by rectal palpation.

## answer_b
Anterior lobe

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The anterior lobe (isthmus) is a thin fibromuscular band in front of the urethra with essentially no glandular tissue and no relation to the ejaculatory ducts.

## answer_c
Median lobe

## explanation_c
Incorrect. The median lobe lies between the urethra and the ejaculatory ducts, not below them against the urogenital diaphragm.

## answer_d
Lateral lobe

## explanation_d
Incorrect. The lateral lobes flank the urethra on either side and form the main bulk of the gland, without this specific inferior/ejaculatory-duct relation.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q017

## title
Spasm of which muscle can cause pain referred to the thigh muscles supplied by the sacral plexus, because the plexus lies directly on its anterior (pelvic) surface?

## question
Spasm of which muscle can cause pain referred to the thigh muscles supplied by the sacral plexus, because the plexus lies directly on its anterior (pelvic) surface?

## subject
msk

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Piriformis

## explanation_a
Correct. The sacral plexus is formed on the anterior surface of piriformis, so spasm or inflammation of piriformis can irritate the plexus (including the sciatic nerve) and produce pain referred to the thigh and buttock muscles the plexus supplies — the basis of piriformis syndrome. This anatomical relationship underlies "piriformis syndrome," where sciatica-like thigh and buttock pain occurs without a true disc lesion.

## answer_b
Obturator internus

## explanation_b
Incorrect. Obturator internus lies on the lateral pelvic wall and forms the lateral wall of the ischiorectal fossa; the sacral plexus does not lie directly on it.

## answer_c
Levator ani

## explanation_c
Incorrect. Levator ani forms the pelvic floor below the plexus, not a surface the plexus lies directly upon.

## answer_d
Coccygeus

## explanation_d
Incorrect. Coccygeus lies posterior to the sacral plexus near the sacrospinous ligament but is not the muscle the plexus rests on.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q019

## title
What structure connects (provides communication between) the vas deferens and the prostatic urethra?

## question
What structure connects (provides communication between) the vas deferens and the prostatic urethra?

## subject
androl

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Ejaculatory duct

## explanation_a
Correct. The ejaculatory duct is formed by the union of the ampulla of the vas deferens with the duct of the seminal vesicle, and it runs through the substance of the prostate to open into the prostatic urethra at the seminal colliculus, providing the anatomical continuity between the vas deferens and the urethra. Recognising the ejaculatory duct as the true conduit, rather than the blind prostatic utricle beside it, avoids a common exam mix-up at the seminal colliculus.

## answer_b
Prostatic utricle

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The prostatic utricle is a small blind pouch opening on the seminal colliculus, a Müllerian remnant, not a conduit connecting the vas deferens to the urethra.

## answer_c
Ductus epididymis

## explanation_c
Incorrect. The ductus (duct) of the epididymis is proximal to the vas deferens, continuous with it, but does not itself connect to the urethra.

## answer_d
Urethral crest

## explanation_d
Incorrect. The urethral crest is a longitudinal ridge on the posterior wall of the prostatic urethra on which the seminal colliculus sits; it is a landmark, not a conduit.

Male reproductive anatomy

Vas deferens and ejaculatory duct

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q020

## title
The spongy (penile) urethra begins within which structure?

## question
The spongy (penile) urethra begins within which structure?

## subject
androl

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
The bulb of the penis (proximal corpus spongiosum)

## explanation_a
Correct. The spongy urethra begins as the membranous urethra pierces the perineal membrane and enters the bulb of the penis, the expanded proximal part of the corpus spongiosum; it then runs the length of the penis within the corpus spongiosum to the external urethral meatus. This is why urethral catheterisation or instrumentation must respect the corpus spongiosum's course rather than assuming the urethra runs within a corpus cavernosum.

## answer_b
The corpus cavernosum

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The paired corpora cavernosa are the erectile bodies dorsal to the spongiosum and do not contain the urethra.

## answer_c
The tunica albuginea

## explanation_c
Incorrect. The tunica albuginea is the fibrous capsule surrounding each erectile body, not a space that contains the urethra.

## answer_d
Buck's (deep penile) fascia

## explanation_d
Incorrect. Buck's fascia is the deep fascia enclosing all three erectile bodies together; it is a covering layer, not the structure the urethra runs within.

## topic
Male reproductive anatomy

## subtopic
Penis and urethra

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q021

## title
Regarding Müllerian-inhibiting hormone (MIH/anti-Müllerian hormone) in the male fetus, which of the following is true?

## question
Regarding Müllerian-inhibiting hormone (MIH/anti-Müllerian hormone) in the male fetus, which of the following is true?

## subject
androl

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
It causes regression of the paramesonephric (Müllerian) ducts

## explanation_a
Correct. Müllerian-inhibiting hormone (anti-Müllerian hormone) is secreted by the Sertoli cells of the fetal testis and causes regression of the paramesonephric (Müllerian) ducts in the male, so that the uterus, uterine tubes and upper vagina do not develop; simultaneously, testosterone from the Leydig cells maintains and virilises the mesonephric (Wolffian) ducts. Loss-of-function mutations in MIH or its receptor cause persistent Müllerian duct syndrome, in which an otherwise normal male retains a uterus and tubes.

## answer_b
It stimulates development of the mesonephric (Wolffian) ducts

## explanation_b
Incorrect. Mesonephric duct development is maintained by testosterone from the fetal Leydig cells, not by MIH.

## answer_c
It is secreted by the Leydig cells of the fetal testis

## explanation_c
Incorrect. MIH is secreted by the Sertoli cells (the supporting cells of the seminiferous tubules), not the steroidogenic Leydig cells.

## answer_d
It stimulates development of the paramesonephric ducts

## explanation_d
Incorrect. MIH suppresses, rather than stimulates, paramesonephric duct development — the opposite of its actual action.

## topic

## subtopic

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q022

## title
The lymphatic drainage of the ovary, the testis, and most of the uterine (fallopian) tube is to which group of lymph nodes?

## question
The lymphatic drainage of the ovary, the testis, and most of the uterine (fallopian) tube is to which group of lymph nodes?

## subject
gyn

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Para-aortic (lumbar) lymph nodes

## explanation_a
Correct. The gonads (ovary and testis) and most of the uterine tube originate high on the posterior abdominal wall during development and descend carrying their blood and lymphatic supply with them; their lymphatics therefore follow the gonadal vessels retroperitoneally to drain into the para-aortic (lumbar) nodes at the level of L1-L2, rather than into the pelvic node groups. This shared retroperitoneal drainage route is exactly why testicular or ovarian cancer staging focuses on the para-aortic nodes rather than the groin.

## answer_b
Superficial inguinal lymph nodes

## explanation_b
Incorrect. Superficial inguinal nodes drain structures of true cutaneous/labioscrotal or lower-limb origin (e.g., vulva, scrotal skin, lower limb), not the gonads themselves.

## answer_c
External iliac lymph nodes

## explanation_c
Incorrect. External iliac nodes mainly drain the cervix, upper vagina and structures along the external iliac vessels, not the gonads.

## answer_d
Internal iliac lymph nodes

## explanation_d
Incorrect. Internal iliac nodes drain most pelvic viscera supplied by internal iliac branches (bladder, rectum, prostate), not the gonads, which drain along their own embryonic vessels.

## topic
Female reproductive anatomy

## subtopic
Pelvic lymphatics

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q024

## title
Which structure is closely related to the lateral fornix of the vagina, placing it at risk of injury during hysterectomy at this level?

## question
Which structure is closely related to the lateral fornix of the vagina, placing it at risk of injury during hysterectomy at this level?

## subject
gyn

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Ureter

## explanation_a
Correct. The ureter passes close to the lateral fornix of the vagina, just below the point where the uterine artery crosses over it ("water under the bridge"); this proximity is why the ureter is vulnerable to injury or ligation during clamping of the uterine vessels at hysterectomy, and can be palpated through the lateral fornix. Recognising this relationship is why surgeons deliberately identify and mobilise the ureter before clamping the uterine vessels in a hysterectomy.

## answer_b
Ovarian artery

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The ovarian artery runs in the suspensory ligament of the ovary, well above the level of the vaginal fornices.

## answer_c
Obturator nerve

## explanation_c
Incorrect. The obturator nerve runs on the lateral pelvic wall toward the obturator canal, away from the vaginal fornices.

## answer_d
Internal iliac vein

## explanation_d
Incorrect. The internal iliac vein lies on the lateral pelvic wall, not directly adjacent to the lateral vaginal fornix.

## subtopic

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q025

## title
The labia minora develop from which embryonic structure?

## question
The labia minora develop from which embryonic structure?

## subject
gyn

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Urethral (urogenital) folds

## explanation_a
Correct. In the absence of high androgen levels, the paired urethral (urogenital) folds remain unfused in the female and form the labia minora, flanking the vestibule; in the male the equivalent folds fuse over the urethral groove to form the ventral shaft skin around the spongy urethra. In males, the same urogenital folds fuse over the urethral groove instead, which is why hypospadias (failure of this fusion) leaves an abnormal urethral opening along the penile shaft.

## answer_b
Labioscrotal (genital) swellings

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The labioscrotal swellings remain unfused in the female to form the labia majora, not the labia minora.

## answer_c
Genital tubercle

## explanation_c
Incorrect. The genital tubercle forms the clitoris (or penis), not the labia minora.

## answer_d
Cloacal membrane

## explanation_d
Incorrect. The cloacal membrane breaks down early to establish the urogenital and anal openings; it is not the direct source of the labia minora.

## topic

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q026

## title
The part of the broad ligament found between the ovary and the uterine (fallopian) tube is called the?

## question
The part of the broad ligament found between the ovary and the uterine (fallopian) tube is called the?

## subject
gyn

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Mesosalpinx

## explanation_a
Correct. The mesosalpinx is the upper free part of the broad ligament lying between the uterine tube (superiorly) and the mesovarium/ovary (inferiorly), forming a short mesentery for the tube. Keeping the three broad-ligament subdivisions straight (mesosalpinx-tube, mesovarium-ovary, mesometrium-uterus) is a frequent source of confusion on pelvic anatomy exams.

## answer_b
Mesovarium

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The mesovarium is the fold of broad ligament that attaches the anterior border of the ovary to the posterior layer of the broad ligament, not the part between the ovary and the tube.

## answer_c
Mesometrium

## explanation_c
Incorrect. The mesometrium is the largest, lowermost part of the broad ligament, related to the body of the uterus.

## answer_d
Suspensory ligament of the ovary

## explanation_d
Incorrect. The suspensory ligament of the ovary is a separate peritoneal fold carrying the ovarian vessels from the pelvic wall to the ovary, not a subdivision of the broad ligament proper between tube and ovary.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q027

## title
The seminal vesicles develop as an outgrowth of which embryonic duct?

## question
The seminal vesicles develop as an outgrowth of which embryonic duct?

## subject
androl

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Mesonephric (Wolffian) duct

## explanation_a
Correct. Under the influence of testosterone, the distal mesonephric (Wolffian) duct gives off a lateral outgrowth just before it reaches the urogenital sinus; this outgrowth becomes the seminal vesicle, while the duct itself becomes the epididymis, vas deferens and ejaculatory duct. Testosterone acting locally (paracrine) on the adjacent mesonephric duct drives this outgrowth, which is why disorders of androgen action can leave the seminal vesicles underdeveloped.

## answer_b
Paramesonephric (Müllerian) duct

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The paramesonephric duct regresses in the male under MIH and does not contribute to the seminal vesicle.

## answer_c
Ureteric bud

## explanation_c
Incorrect. The ureteric bud is a separate outgrowth of the mesonephric duct that forms the ureter, renal pelvis and collecting system, not the seminal vesicle.

## answer_d
Urogenital sinus

## explanation_d
Incorrect. The urogenital sinus forms the bladder and parts of the urethra; the seminal vesicle buds from the mesonephric duct near, but proximal to, the sinus.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q028

## title
The labia majora drain lymph primarily to which group of nodes?

## question
The labia majora drain lymph primarily to which group of nodes?

## subject
gyn

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Superficial inguinal lymph nodes

## explanation_a
Correct. The labia majora, developing from the labioscrotal swellings (homologous to the scrotum), retain a cutaneous-type lymphatic drainage to the superficial inguinal lymph nodes, the same first-echelon nodes that drain the scrotal skin and perineum. This is the anatomical basis for why vulvar carcinoma is staged primarily by groin (inguinal) lymph node involvement rather than pelvic node involvement.

## answer_b
Para-aortic lymph nodes

## explanation_b
Incorrect. Para-aortic nodes drain the gonads (ovary/testis), which follow a separate, retroperitoneal embryological drainage route unlike the labia majora.

## answer_c
External iliac lymph nodes

## explanation_c
Incorrect. External iliac nodes drain deeper pelvic structures such as the cervix and upper vagina, not the vulval skin.

## answer_d
Deep inguinal lymph nodes

## explanation_d
Incorrect. Deep inguinal nodes receive efferents from the superficial inguinal nodes and the glans clitoris/penis, not the primary drainage site for the labia majora as a whole.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q029

## title
Failure of development (aplasia) of one paramesonephric duct, with normal development of the other, results in which uterine anomaly?

## question
Failure of development (aplasia) of one paramesonephric duct, with normal development of the other, results in which uterine anomaly?

## subject
gyn

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Unicornuate uterus (uterus unicornis)

## explanation_a
Correct. If one paramesonephric duct fails to develop while the other develops normally, only one horn and one uterine tube form, producing a unicornuate uterus, often with a single functioning ovary/tube on that side and a rudimentary or absent horn on the other. A unicornuate uterus is clinically important because it is associated with a higher risk of infertility, ectopic pregnancy and preterm labour than a normally fused uterus.

## answer_b
Uterus didelphys

## explanation_b
Incorrect. Uterus didelphys results from complete failure of fusion of two otherwise normally developed paramesonephric ducts, producing two separate uteri and cervices, not from aplasia of one duct.

## answer_c
Bicornuate uterus

## explanation_c
Incorrect. A bicornuate uterus results from partial failure of fusion of the two ducts (both present, incompletely joined), not from loss of one duct.

## answer_d
Septate uterus

## explanation_d
Incorrect. A septate uterus results from failure of resorption of the midline septum after otherwise normal fusion, with both ducts present and fused externally.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q030

## title
Which of the following is NOT covered by peritoneum?

## question
Which of the following is NOT covered by peritoneum?

## subject
gyn

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Anterior wall of the (supravaginal) cervix

## explanation_a
Correct. The peritoneum reflects forward off the isthmus of the uterus onto the bladder to form the uterovesical pouch, so the anterior surface of the supravaginal cervix (and the upper vagina) is left bare of peritoneum, separated from the bladder by loose connective tissue rather than a peritoneal layer. This peritoneal gap is also the surgical plane exploited when the bladder is mobilised off the cervix during a hysterectomy.

## answer_b
Posterior wall of the cervix

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The posterior wall of the cervix, along with the posterior fornix of the vagina, is covered by peritoneum that dips down to form the recto-uterine pouch (of Douglas).

## answer_c
Fundus of the uterus

## explanation_c
Incorrect. The fundus of the uterus is covered by peritoneum on all its surfaces, continuous with the broad ligament laterally.

## answer_d
Posterior wall of the uterine body

## explanation_d
Incorrect. The posterior wall of the uterine body is covered by peritoneum, forming the anterior wall of the recto-uterine pouch.

## topic

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q031

## title
Which part of the male urethra is its widest and most distensible part?

## question
Which part of the male urethra is its widest and most distensible part?

## subject
androl

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Prostatic urethra

## explanation_a
Correct. The prostatic urethra, running through the substance of the prostate, is the widest and most dilatable segment of the male urethra; the seminal colliculus projects into its posterior wall. This distensibility is clinically relevant because prostatic urethral strictures are less common than membranous or bulbar strictures, which occur at the narrower, more fixed segments.

## answer_b
Membranous urethra

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The membranous urethra, surrounded by the external urethral sphincter as it crosses the deep perineal pouch, is the narrowest and least distensible part of the urethra.

## answer_c
Bulbar (spongy) urethra

## explanation_c
Incorrect. The bulbar urethra within the corpus spongiosum is wider than the membranous urethra but not as wide as the prostatic urethra.

## answer_d
Navicular fossa

## explanation_d
Incorrect. The navicular fossa is a small dilation just proximal to the external meatus, not the widest overall segment.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q032

## title
The uterine (fallopian) tube develops from which embryonic structure?

## question
The uterine (fallopian) tube develops from which embryonic structure?

## subject
gyn

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Paramesonephric (Müllerian) duct

## explanation_a
Correct. The unfused cranial (upper) part of each paramesonephric duct becomes a uterine tube, opening into the peritoneal cavity at its funnel-shaped fimbriated end, while the fused caudal parts of the two ducts form the uterus and upper vagina. The caudal, fused parts of the two ducts instead form the uterus and upper vagina, so a single embryonic duct system gives rise to the entire female genital tract from tube to upper vagina.

## answer_b
Mesonephric (Wolffian) duct

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The mesonephric duct regresses in the female (persisting only as vestigial remnants such as Gartner's duct) and does not form the uterine tube.

## answer_c
Urogenital sinus

## explanation_c
Incorrect. The urogenital sinus forms the bladder, urethra and lower vagina, not the uterine tube.

## answer_d
Genital ridge

## explanation_d
Incorrect. The genital ridge gives rise to the gonad (ovary/testis) itself, not the duct system.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q033

## title
Sensory innervation of the lower third of the vagina is supplied mainly by which nerve?

## question
Sensory innervation of the lower third of the vagina is supplied mainly by which nerve?

## subject
gyn

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Pudendal nerve

## explanation_a
Correct. The lower third of the vagina, derived from the urogenital sinus, is somatically innervated (touch, pain, temperature) by the pudendal nerve (S2-S4), the same nerve that supplies the perineum and external genitalia, which is why this part of the vagina is sensate. This somatic-versus-visceral split in vaginal innervation is also why procedures on the lower vagina need local anaesthesia while upper vaginal/cervical manipulation is comparatively painless without it.

## answer_b
Hypogastric (uterovaginal) autonomic plexus

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The upper two-thirds of the vagina, of paramesonephric origin, is supplied by the visceral (autonomic) uterovaginal plexus and is relatively insensitive to touch, unlike the pudendal-innervated lower third.

## answer_c
Ilioinguinal nerve

## explanation_c
Incorrect. The ilioinguinal nerve supplies the upper medial thigh and adjacent labial skin, not the vaginal wall itself.

## answer_d
Genitofemoral nerve

## explanation_d
Incorrect. The genital branch of the genitofemoral nerve supplies the labia majora/mons skin, not the lower vaginal mucosa.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q034

## title
Anabolic androgens (e.g., nandrolone) have historically been used therapeutically for which of the following indications?

## question
Anabolic androgens (e.g., nandrolone) have historically been used therapeutically for which of the following indications?

## subject
pharm

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Osteoporosis

## explanation_a
Correct. Anabolic androgens increase bone matrix formation and muscle mass, and were historically used to help manage osteoporosis (particularly in older or debilitated patients); this use has been largely superseded by bisphosphonates, SERMs and other agents with fewer androgenic side effects, but it remains the classically taught indication. Modern practice favours bisphosphonates or SERMs for osteoporosis because they lack the virilising and cardiovascular side effects that limit long-term anabolic androgen use.

## answer_b
Acute intermittent porphyria

## explanation_b
Incorrect. Acute intermittent porphyria is managed with hemin and glucose, not anabolic androgens (androgens can in fact precipitate porphyric attacks).

## answer_c
Hypercalcemia of malignancy

## explanation_c
Incorrect. Hypercalcemia of malignancy is treated with isotonic saline, bisphosphonates or calcitonin, not androgens.

## answer_d
Precocious puberty

## explanation_d
Incorrect. Precocious puberty is treated by suppressing, not adding, sex-steroid activity (e.g., with GnRH agonists), the opposite of giving an anabolic androgen.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q035

## title
Finasteride, a 5-alpha-reductase inhibitor used in benign prostatic hyperplasia, acts mainly by which mechanism?

## question
Finasteride, a 5-alpha-reductase inhibitor used in benign prostatic hyperplasia, acts mainly by which mechanism?

## subject
pharm

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Decreasing conversion of testosterone to dihydrotestosterone (DHT)

## explanation_a
Correct. Finasteride inhibits type II 5-alpha-reductase, the enzyme that converts testosterone to the more potent androgen dihydrotestosterone (DHT) within the prostate; because DHT is the main driver of prostatic glandular growth, lowering DHT shrinks the gland over months and slowly relieves obstructive BPH symptoms. Because finasteride acts on gland volume rather than smooth-muscle tone, patients are counselled that maximal symptom benefit may take three to six months to appear.

## answer_b
Blocking alpha-1 adrenergic receptors in prostatic smooth muscle

## explanation_b
Incorrect. Blocking alpha-1 receptors in prostatic smooth muscle is the mechanism of the alpha-blockers (e.g., tamsulosin), not finasteride.

## answer_c
Antagonizing androgen receptors directly

## explanation_c
Incorrect. Direct androgen-receptor antagonism is the mechanism of agents like flutamide or bicalutamide, not finasteride, which acts upstream at the enzyme that generates the receptor's main ligand.

## answer_d
Inhibiting aromatase-mediated conversion of testosterone to estrogen

## explanation_d
Incorrect. Finasteride does not affect aromatase or estrogen synthesis; it is specific to 5-alpha-reductase.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q036

## title
Which of the following is NOT an adverse effect typically associated with parenteral (injectable) testosterone ester therapy in a boy?

## question
Which of the following is NOT an adverse effect typically associated with parenteral (injectable) testosterone ester therapy in a boy?

## subject
pharm

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Hepatotoxicity

## explanation_a
Correct. Hepatotoxicity (cholestatic jaundice, peliosis hepatis) is characteristically a risk of the orally active, 17-alpha-alkylated androgens (e.g., methyltestosterone), which resist first-pass hepatic metabolism; parenteral testosterone esters largely bypass this first-pass exposure and are not typically associated with significant liver toxicity. This is why hepatotoxicity monitoring is emphasised for oral androgen formulations but not routinely required for testosterone injections or transdermal preparations.

## answer_b
Premature closure of the epiphyseal growth plates

## explanation_b
Incorrect. Excess androgen accelerates epiphyseal maturation and can cause premature closure of the growth plates, stunting final height in a boy — a genuine risk of parenteral testosterone.

## answer_c
Priapism

## explanation_c
Incorrect. Priapism (prolonged, painful erection) is a recognized adverse effect of androgen therapy, including parenteral testosterone.

## answer_d
Gynecomastia (from peripheral aromatization to estrogen)

## explanation_d
Incorrect. Peripheral aromatization of exogenous testosterone to estradiol can cause gynecomastia, a genuine adverse effect of testosterone therapy by any route.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q037

## title
Which drug affords rapid, direct symptomatic relief of lower urinary tract symptoms in BPH by relaxing prostatic and bladder-neck smooth muscle, and is licensed for this use?

## question
Which drug affords rapid, direct symptomatic relief of lower urinary tract symptoms in BPH by relaxing prostatic and bladder-neck smooth muscle, and is licensed for this use?

## subject
pharm

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Tadalafil

## explanation_a
Correct. Tadalafil, a long-acting PDE5 inhibitor, is licensed for daily use in BPH-related lower urinary tract symptoms; by increasing cGMP it relaxes smooth muscle in the prostate, bladder neck and detrusor, giving symptomatic relief within days, faster than the months needed for the 5-alpha-reductase inhibitors to shrink the gland. Because tadalafil shares the PDE5-inhibitor drug class with erectile dysfunction agents, prescribers must still screen for concurrent nitrate use before starting it for BPH.

## answer_b
Finasteride

## explanation_b
Incorrect. Finasteride works by shrinking glandular volume over several months via DHT reduction, not by providing rapid smooth-muscle relaxation.

## answer_c
Dutasteride

## explanation_c
Incorrect. Dutasteride, like finasteride, is a 5-alpha-reductase inhibitor with the same slow, volume-reduction mechanism, not a fast symptomatic agent.

## answer_d
Doxazosin

## explanation_d
Incorrect. Doxazosin, an alpha-1 blocker, is also a fast-acting BPH agent, but the printed source names tadalafil specifically for this item.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q038

## title
Leuprolide, a GnRH agonist analogue used in prostate cancer, produces its therapeutic effect mainly through which mechanism?

## question
Leuprolide, a GnRH agonist analogue used in prostate cancer, produces its therapeutic effect mainly through which mechanism?

## subject
pharm

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Continuous (non-pulsatile) receptor stimulation, downregulating pituitary GnRH receptors and suppressing gonadotropin release

## explanation_a
Correct. Unlike the normal pulsatile release of endogenous GnRH, leuprolide is given continuously; this constant receptor occupancy paradoxically downregulates pituitary GnRH receptors after an initial "flare" of LH/FSH release, ultimately suppressing gonadotropin secretion and driving testosterone down to castrate levels, achieving medical (chemical) castration. This is why anti-androgen "flare cover" is co-administered for the first few weeks of GnRH agonist therapy in men with bulky metastatic prostate cancer, to blunt the initial testosterone surge.

## answer_b
Direct blockade of androgen receptors in prostatic tissue

## explanation_b
Incorrect. Direct androgen-receptor blockade is the mechanism of anti-androgens such as bicalutamide, an agent often combined with, not equivalent to, a GnRH agonist.

## answer_c
Inhibition of 5-alpha-reductase

## explanation_c
Incorrect. 5-alpha-reductase inhibition is the mechanism of finasteride/dutasteride, unrelated to GnRH receptor pharmacology.

## answer_d
Direct inhibition of testicular steroidogenesis independent of the pituitary

## explanation_d
Incorrect. Leuprolide acts centrally at the pituitary (and hypothalamic axis); it does not directly inhibit testicular Leydig-cell steroidogenesis independent of gonadotropin suppression.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q039

## title
Visual disturbances (e.g., blue-tinged vision, altered colour perception) are a recognized adverse effect of PDE5 inhibitors because of cross-reactivity with which enzyme?

## question
Visual disturbances (e.g., blue-tinged vision, altered colour perception) are a recognized adverse effect of PDE5 inhibitors because of cross-reactivity with which enzyme?

## subject
pharm

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
PDE6 (retinal phosphodiesterase)

## explanation_a
Correct. PDE5 inhibitors are not perfectly selective and show some cross-reactivity with PDE6, the phosphodiesterase isoform found in retinal photoreceptors that is part of the phototransduction cascade; inhibiting PDE6 alters visual signal processing and produces the characteristic transient blue-tinged vision and light sensitivity, more prominent with sildenafil than with tadalafil. Sildenafil has a higher affinity for PDE6 than tadalafil, which is why blue-tinted vision is reported more often with sildenafil than with the other PDE5 inhibitors.

## answer_b
PDE3 (cardiac/vascular phosphodiesterase)

## explanation_b
Incorrect. PDE3 inhibition affects cardiac contractility and vascular tone (relevant to drug interactions with nitrates via a different pathway), not the visual effect.

## answer_c
PDE4 (inflammatory-cell phosphodiesterase)

## explanation_c
Incorrect. PDE4 is mainly expressed in inflammatory and airway smooth-muscle cells; it is not implicated in the visual side effect.

## answer_d
Soluble guanylate cyclase

## explanation_d
Incorrect. Guanylate cyclase generates cGMP upstream of PDE5 action; it is not the enzyme responsible for the cross-reactivity that causes visual disturbance.

## topic
Male reproductive pharmacology

## subtopic

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q040

## title
Raloxifene, a selective estrogen receptor modulator (SERM), is used clinically for which of the following?

## question
Raloxifene, a selective estrogen receptor modulator (SERM), is used clinically for which of the following?

## subject
pharm

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Prevention and treatment of postmenopausal osteoporosis

## explanation_a
Correct. Raloxifene acts as an estrogen-receptor agonist on bone (reducing resorption and increasing density, so is approved for prevention and treatment of postmenopausal osteoporosis) while acting as an antagonist on breast and endometrial tissue, giving it a tissue-selective SERM profile distinct from either estrogen or tamoxifen. This tissue-selective profile is why raloxifene is sometimes described as a "designer estrogen" that captures bone benefit while avoiding breast and endometrial stimulation.

## answer_b
Treatment of menopausal hot flashes

## explanation_b
Incorrect. Raloxifene does not relieve, and can even worsen, vasomotor symptoms such as hot flashes, unlike hormone replacement therapy.

## answer_c
Endometrial stimulation alongside estrogen replacement therapy

## explanation_c
Incorrect. Raloxifene is an estrogen-receptor antagonist in the endometrium (unlike tamoxifen), so it does not stimulate endometrial proliferation; this is part of why it avoids tamoxifen's endometrial cancer risk.

## answer_d
Treatment of active, metastatic breast cancer

## explanation_d
Incorrect. Raloxifene is used to reduce the risk of invasive breast cancer in high-risk women, not to treat established active or metastatic breast cancer, for which tamoxifen or aromatase inhibitors are used instead.

## topic
Female reproductive pharmacology

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q041

## title
Tadalafil is contraindicated in patients taking which class of drugs, because of the risk of severe, potentially life-threatening hypotension?

## question
Tadalafil is contraindicated in patients taking which class of drugs, because of the risk of severe, potentially life-threatening hypotension?

## subject
pharm

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Nitrates

## explanation_a
Correct. Both nitrates and PDE5 inhibitors such as tadalafil raise intracellular cGMP in vascular smooth muscle (nitrates by donating nitric oxide, tadalafil by preventing cGMP breakdown); co-administration causes an exaggerated, synergistic fall in blood pressure that can be severe and life-threatening, so concurrent use is contraindicated. Patients are specifically counselled to disclose PDE5 inhibitor use to any physician considering nitrate therapy (e.g., for chest pain), since the interaction can be fatal even with short-acting nitrates taken hours apart.

## answer_b
Beta-blockers

## explanation_b
Incorrect. Beta-blockers are not absolutely contraindicated with tadalafil, though additional blood-pressure lowering should be monitored; the mechanism-specific, dangerous interaction is with nitrates.

## answer_c
Thiazide diuretics

## explanation_c
Incorrect. Thiazide diuretics can contribute to hypotension in general but do not share the specific cGMP-mediated synergistic mechanism that makes the nitrate interaction dangerous.

## answer_d
ACE inhibitors

## explanation_d
Incorrect. ACE inhibitors are used cautiously with PDE5 inhibitors but are not the classic absolute contraindication taught for this drug class.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q042

## title
Which adverse effect of combined oral contraceptives (OCPs) is attributed to the estrogen component rather than the progestin?

## question
Which adverse effect of combined oral contraceptives (OCPs) is attributed to the estrogen component rather than the progestin?

## subject
pharm

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
A small increase in breast cancer risk

## explanation_a
Correct. A small increase in breast (and cervical) cancer risk associated with combined OCP use is attributed mainly to the estrogenic component's proliferative effect on hormone-sensitive tissue, in contrast with several other listed adverse effects, which are more closely tied to the progestin component of older formulations. Because this risk is small and the absolute numbers are debated, clinicians weigh it against OCPs' well-established reduction in ovarian and endometrial cancer risk when counselling patients.

## answer_b
Acne and hirsutism

## explanation_b
Incorrect. Acne and hirsutism are androgenic side effects driven by progestins with androgenic activity (e.g., older second-generation progestins), not by the estrogen component.

## answer_c
Weight gain and fluid retention

## explanation_c
Incorrect. Weight gain and fluid retention with older, higher-dose formulations were mainly attributed to the progestin's mineralocorticoid-like/androgenic activity, not the estrogen.

## answer_d
Depression and mood changes

## explanation_d
Incorrect. Mood changes and depression have classically been attributed more to the progestin component of combined OCPs.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q043

## title
Which of the following is true regarding the peripheral zone of the prostate?

## question
Which of the following is true regarding the peripheral zone of the prostate?

## subject
androl

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Contains corpora amylacea

## explanation_a
Correct. The peripheral zone, which makes up roughly 70% of prostatic glandular tissue and gives rise to the majority of prostate carcinomas, characteristically contains corpora amylacea — eosinophilic, concentrically laminated proteinaceous concretions that form within gland lumina and increase in number and size with advancing age. Their presence is a normal histologic feature of aging peripheral-zone acini rather than a marker of disease, and they are a classic teaching point distinguishing peripheral from central and transition zone tissue.

## answer_b
Is the most common site of origin of BPH nodules

## explanation_b
Incorrect. Benign prostatic hyperplasia nodules characteristically arise in the transition zone surrounding the urethra, not the peripheral zone, which is why BPH and peripheral-zone carcinoma can coexist without one causing the other.

## answer_c
Is relatively resistant to both carcinoma and inflammatory processes

## explanation_c
Incorrect. It is the central zone, not the peripheral zone, that is relatively resistant to both carcinoma and inflammatory prostatitis.

## answer_d
Is lined by a single layer of secretory cells with no basal cell layer

## explanation_d
Incorrect. Normal prostatic glandular epithelium is composed of two cell layers — a luminal secretory columnar layer overlying a discontinuous basal cell layer — and loss of this basal layer is a key histologic clue to adenocarcinoma.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q044

## title
Which of the following is true regarding Sertoli cells?

## question
Which of the following is true regarding Sertoli cells?

## subject
androl

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Have apical cytoplasmic infoldings with an indistinct lateral cell border

## explanation_a
Correct. Sertoli cells are tall, irregularly shaped supporting cells extending from the basement membrane to the lumen of the seminiferous tubule; their lateral and apical plasma membranes form complex, deeply interdigitating folds around developing germ cells, which is why their cell boundaries appear indistinct on routine light microscopy. This irregular, folded architecture lets a single Sertoli cell physically support and nurture many germ cells at different stages of spermatogenesis simultaneously.

## answer_b
Are the primary source of testosterone within the testis

## explanation_b
Incorrect. Testosterone is produced by Leydig (interstitial) cells lying between seminiferous tubules, not by Sertoli cells, although Sertoli cells concentrate testosterone via androgen-binding protein to support spermatogenesis.

## answer_c
Undergo continuous mitotic division throughout adult life

## explanation_c
Incorrect. Sertoli cells are terminally differentiated after puberty and essentially do not divide in the adult testis; it is the spermatogonia at the base of the epithelium that continue to divide.

## answer_d
Have their tight junctions located outside the tubule wall, at the interstitium

## explanation_d
Incorrect. The tight junctions that form the blood-testis barrier lie between adjacent Sertoli cells near the base of the seminiferous epithelium, not at the outer boundary between the tubule and the interstitium.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q045

## title
Which part of the spermatozoon is surrounded by mitochondria?

## question
Which part of the spermatozoon is surrounded by mitochondria?

## subject
androl

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
B

## answer_a
Head

## explanation_a
Incorrect. The head contains the condensed nucleus and the acrosomal cap, not a mitochondrial sheath.

## answer_b
Middle piece

## explanation_b
Correct. The middle piece of the sperm tail contains the axoneme wrapped in a helical sheath of mitochondria arranged end-to-end. This generate the ATP needed to power flagellar beating and sperm motility.

## answer_c
Principal piece

## explanation_c
Incorrect. The principal piece, the longest segment of the tail, is surrounded by a fibrous sheath rather than mitochondria.

## answer_d
End piece

## explanation_d
Incorrect. The end piece is the short terminal segment containing only the axoneme, without an organized mitochondrial or fibrous sheath.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q046

## title
The vas deferens is characterized by which of the following features?

## question
The vas deferens is characterized by which of the following features?

## subject
androl

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
It has a thick, three-layered muscularis

## explanation_a
Correct. The vas deferens is characterized by an unusually thick three-layered smooth muscle coat — inner longitudinal, middle circular, and outer longitudinal — surrounding a narrow, irregular lumen. This thick musculosa is the single most useful histologic feature for identifying the vas deferens and provides the strong peristaltic contractions that propel sperm during ejaculation.

## answer_b
It has the widest lumen of any part of the male excretory duct system

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The vas deferens lumen is narrow and irregular in outline; it is the epididymis, particularly its proximal portion, that has a wider, more regular lumen.

## answer_c
It lacks a muscularis and relies on ciliary transport of sperm

## explanation_c
Incorrect. The vas deferens is a highly muscular duct whose thick wall generates the peristaltic contractions responsible for expelling sperm at emission, not a passive ciliary conduit.

## answer_d
Its epithelium and wall are histologically identical to the epididymis

## explanation_d
Incorrect. While the vas deferens shares a pseudostratified columnar epithelium with stereocilia similar to the epididymis, what distinguishes it is precisely its markedly thicker three-layered muscular wall, which the epididymal duct lacks.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q048

## title
Sertoli cells help maintain a supply of high levels of local testosterone by which of the following?

## question
Sertoli cells help maintain a supply of high levels of local testosterone by which of the following?

## subject
androl

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
B

## answer_a
Synthesizing testosterone de novo from cholesterol

## explanation_a
Incorrect. Testosterone is synthesized de novo from cholesterol by Leydig cells, not Sertoli cells; Sertoli cells instead concentrate testosterone that Leydig cells have already produced.

## answer_b
Secreting androgen-binding protein (ABP)

## explanation_b
Correct. Sertoli cells secrete androgen-binding protein (ABP) into the seminiferous tubule lumen; ABP binds testosterone and keeps it at a high local concentration within the tubular fluid, which is necessary to sustain the later stages of spermatogenesis. ABP secretion is stimulated by FSH acting on Sertoli cells.

## answer_c
Converting testosterone to dihydrotestosterone via 5-alpha reductase

## explanation_c
Incorrect. Conversion of testosterone to the more potent dihydrotestosterone by 5-alpha reductase occurs mainly in peripheral androgen target tissues such as the prostate and skin, and is not the mechanism by which Sertoli cells raise intratubular testosterone.

## answer_d
Secreting inhibin, which directly raises testosterone levels

## explanation_d
Incorrect. Inhibin acts on the anterior pituitary to suppress FSH secretion; it does not directly raise testosterone levels.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q049

## title
Which of the following is true regarding the CatSper protein?

## question
Which of the following is true regarding the CatSper protein?

## subject
androl

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
It is present in the plasma membrane of the sperm tail

## explanation_a
Correct. CatSper is a sperm-specific calcium channel located in the plasma membrane of the principal piece of the tail. Calcium influx through CatSper drives the whip-like, high-amplitude flagellar beating known as hyperactivated motility, which sperm need to penetrate the cumulus oophorus and zona pellucida.

## answer_b
It is located in the acrosome and mediates the acrosome reaction

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The acrosome reaction is mediated by a distinct set of membrane receptors and exocytotic machinery in the sperm head, not by CatSper, which is confined to the tail.

## answer_c
It is a mitochondrial protein of the middle piece involved in ATP synthesis

## explanation_c
Incorrect. ATP synthesis in the middle piece is carried out by the mitochondrial sheath, a structurally and functionally distinct component from the CatSper channel.

## answer_d
It is a nuclear protein that compacts sperm chromatin

## explanation_d
Incorrect. Sperm chromatin compaction is achieved by protamines replacing histones in the nucleus, an unrelated process to the CatSper tail-membrane channel.

## topic
Male reproductive histology

## subtopic
Spermatogenesis

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q050

## title
What is the lining of the intralobular and smaller interlobular ducts of the resting breast?

## question
What is the lining of the intralobular and smaller interlobular ducts of the resting breast?

## subject
gyn

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Two layers of cuboidal cells with an underlying myoepithelial layer

## explanation_a
Correct. In the resting breast, the intralobular and smaller interlobular ducts are lined by two layers of cuboidal epithelial cells with an underlying, discontinuous layer of myoepithelial cells. This contract to aid fluid movement and provide a supportive basal layer.

## answer_b
A single layer of tall columnar secretory cells without myoepithelial cells

## explanation_b
Incorrect. A single layer of tall secretory columnar cells describes the alveolar epithelium of the actively lactating breast, not the resting ducts, and it too retains an outer myoepithelial layer.

## answer_c
Stratified squamous epithelium continuous with the nipple skin

## explanation_c
Incorrect. Stratified squamous epithelium is found only at the nipple surface and the most proximal segment of the lactiferous duct near its orifice, not throughout the intralobular and interlobular ducts.

## answer_d
Simple squamous epithelium

## explanation_d
Incorrect. Simple squamous epithelium is not a normal lining of the breast ductal system at any level.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q051

## title
Which of the following is a characteristic histologic feature of the fallopian tube, particularly its ampulla?

## question
Which of the following is a characteristic histologic feature of the fallopian tube, particularly its ampulla?

## subject
gyn

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Its mucosa is thrown into complex primary, secondary, and tertiary folds

## explanation_a
Correct. The fallopian tube mucosa is elaborately folded into primary, secondary, and even tertiary plicae, an arrangement most complex in the ampulla. The folds are lined by simple columnar epithelium containing both ciliated cells, which help transport the oocyte, and secretory (peg) cells.

## answer_b
It has a smooth, unfolded mucosa lined by simple squamous epithelium

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The tubal mucosa is highly folded, not smooth, and is lined by simple columnar epithelium, not simple squamous epithelium.

## answer_c
It has a thick keratinized stratified squamous lining similar to the vagina

## explanation_c
Incorrect. A thick keratinized stratified squamous lining is characteristic of the vagina and ectocervix, not the fallopian tube.

## answer_d
It has glandular endometrial-type mucosa with spiral arteries

## explanation_d
Incorrect. Glandular mucosa with spiral arteries describes the uterine endometrium, a structurally distinct organ from the fallopian tube.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q053

## title
Arrest and failure of chromosome separation during the first meiotic division of a primary oocyte most typically results in which of the following?

## question
Arrest and failure of chromosome separation during the first meiotic division of a primary oocyte most typically results in which of the following?

## subject
gyn

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
B

## answer_a
Turner syndrome

## explanation_a
Incorrect. Turner syndrome (45,X) most often arises from loss of a sex chromosome, frequently of paternal origin or through a post-zygotic mitotic error, rather than being the classic teaching consequence of maternal meiosis I nondisjunction.

## answer_b
Down syndrome

## explanation_b
Correct. Human primary oocytes remain arrested in prophase of the first meiotic division from before birth until ovulation, sometimes for decades. This prolonged arrest is thought to predispose to nondisjunction — failure of homologous chromosome pairs to separate correctly — which is the classic mechanism producing trisomy 21 (Down syndrome), with risk rising sharply with advancing maternal age.

## answer_c
Klinefelter syndrome

## explanation_c
Incorrect. While Klinefelter syndrome (47,XXY) can also arise from meiotic nondisjunction, it is trisomy 21 that is the standard teaching example most closely linked to maternal meiosis I errors and advanced maternal age.

## answer_d
A balanced reciprocal translocation carrier state

## explanation_d
Incorrect. A balanced translocation is a structural chromosomal rearrangement transmitted from a carrier parent, an entirely different mechanism from numerical nondisjunction during meiosis.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q054

## title
Which of the following induces penile erection?

## question
Which of the following induces penile erection?

## subject
androl

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Increased cGMP and nitric oxide (NO) release from cavernosal nerve endings and endothelium

## explanation_a
Correct. Parasympathetic stimulation releases nitric oxide from cavernosal nerve endings and vascular endothelium, which activates guanylate cyclase in cavernosal smooth muscle to raise intracellular cyclic GMP; cGMP lowers intracellular calcium and relaxes the smooth muscle of the corpora cavernosa and their arterioles, allowing blood inflow and veno-occlusive trapping that produces erection. This is also the target of phosphodiesterase type 5 inhibitors, which prolong cGMP action by blocking its breakdown.

## answer_b
Increased sympathetic norepinephrine release causing cavernosal artery vasoconstriction

## explanation_b
Incorrect. Sympathetic activity, via norepinephrine on alpha-adrenergic receptors, maintains cavernosal smooth muscle contraction and penile flaccidity; withdrawal of sympathetic tone, not an increase, permits erection.

## answer_c
Increased circulating angiotensin II acting on cavernosal smooth muscle

## explanation_c
Incorrect. Angiotensin II is a potent vasoconstrictor that would oppose, not produce, the smooth muscle relaxation required for erection.

## answer_d
Increased calcium influx into cavernosal smooth muscle

## explanation_d
Incorrect. Erection depends on a fall, not a rise, in cavernosal smooth muscle intracellular calcium; increased calcium influx would instead promote contraction and detumescence.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q055

## title
Which of the following statements about the scrotum is true?

## question
Which of the following statements about the scrotum is true?

## subject
androl

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
It keeps testicular temperature cooler than core body temperature, favoring spermatogenesis

## explanation_a
Correct. The scrotum actively maintains testicular temperature roughly 2-3°C below core body temperature, through the contractile dartos and cremaster muscles that adjust testicular position relative to the body and the pampiniform venous plexus, which cools incoming arterial blood by countercurrent exchange. This cooler environment is required for normal spermatogenesis, which is impaired at core body temperature.

## answer_b
It has no independent thermoregulatory mechanism and simply follows ambient temperature

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The scrotum has active thermoregulatory mechanisms — the dartos and cremaster muscles and the pampiniform plexus — rather than passively following ambient temperature alone.

## answer_c
Scrotal temperature is normally 2-3°C higher than core body temperature

## explanation_c
Incorrect. Scrotal/testicular temperature is normally lower, not higher, than core body temperature; a temperature elevation, as in cryptorchidism, impairs spermatogenesis.

## answer_d
The pampiniform plexus warms incoming testicular arterial blood before it reaches the testis

## explanation_d
Incorrect. The pampiniform plexus cools incoming testicular arterial blood by countercurrent heat exchange with the cooler venous blood leaving the testis, rather than warming it.

# Item

## id
QST-ASUUG-EOMCOLL-Q056

## title
Which of the following represents a function of the prostate?

## question
Which of the following represents a function of the prostate?

## subject
androl

## status
Draft

## owner
Claude

## vignette

## correct_answer
A

## answer_a
Secretes a thin, milky, alkaline fluid

## explanation_a
Correct. The prostate secretes a thin, milky, alkaline fluid containing prostatic acid phosphatase, citrate, zinc, and prostate-specific antigen (PSA). This alkaline fluid helps neutralize the acidic vaginal and residual urethral environment and, through PSA-mediated proteolysis, contributes to liquefaction of the coagulated ejaculate.

## answer_b
Produces the bulk of seminal fructose that nourishes sperm

## explanation_b
Incorrect. The bulk of seminal fructose, the main energy substrate for ejaculated sperm, is produced by the seminal vesicles, not the prostate.

## answer_c
Secretes an acidic fluid that activates sperm motility

## explanation_c
Incorrect. Prostatic fluid is alkaline, not acidic, and sperm motility activation classically occurs after mixing of seminal vesicle and prostatic secretions in the ejaculate, not within the prostate alone.

## answer_d
Is the primary site of spermatozoa storage before ejaculation

## explanation_d
Incorrect. Spermatozoa are stored mainly in the epididymis (and to some extent the ampulla of the vas deferens) before ejaculation, not in the prostate.
